        Idea:
Fix all 5 architectural concerns in the video-editor effect system: (1) Three effect sources merged silently — make migration path explicit or deprecate legacy DB path, (2) Global singleton registry — make reactive so components always use current registry state, (3) No runtime error boundary around effects — add safety net for buggy/AI-generated custom effects, (4) EffectCreatorPanel preview divergence — make preview composition more faithful to actual timeline rendering, (5) Untyped params — add runtime validation or default-filling from parameterSchema at apply-time. Key files: effects/index.tsx, DynamicEffectRegistry.ts, useEffectRegistry.ts, VisualClip.tsx, TextClip.tsx, EffectLayerSequence.tsx, EffectCreatorPanel.tsx, ParameterControls.tsx, types/index.ts

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Fix 5 Architectural Gaps in Video-Editor Effect System

## Overview

The video-editor effect system has five architectural gaps: (1) three effect sources merge silently with no dedup/priority, (2) the registry is non-reactive so components don't re-render when async registration completes, (3) no runtime error boundary for custom effects, (4) EffectCreatorPanel preview diverges from timeline wrapping pipeline, and (5) effect params are unvalidated `Record<string, unknown>` at apply-time.

**Architecture understanding:** `useEffectRegistry` (called in `VideoEditorProvider.tsx:21`) creates a provider-local `DynamicEffectRegistry` via `useMemo()` and mirrors it into a module-level singleton via `replaceEffectRegistry()` inside `useEffect()`. All render-time lookups (`lookupEffect`, `wrapWithClipEffects`, `EffectLayerSequence`) read from `getEffectRegistry()` — the module singleton. Remotion compositions re-render every frame via `useCurrentFrame()`, so once the module singleton is updated, clips pick up changes on the next frame. The core timing issue is that `replaceEffectRegistry()` runs in `useEffect()` (post-render), so the first render may hit a stale default registry.

**Scope:** `CustomEffectEditor.tsx` has no callers in the codebase (dead code) — excluded from this plan. The `customEffects` field in serialization/migration (`serialize.ts`, `migrate.ts`, `timeline-data.ts`) is a broader persistence concern outside the scope of these 5 architectural fixes.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Reactive Registry + Error Boundary

### Step 1: Make DynamicEffectRegistry reactive and fix first-render timing (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts`, `hooks/useEffectRegistry.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** to `DynamicEffectRegistry` class (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts`):
   - Private `_version: number = 0` and `_listeners: Set<() => void> = new Set()`
   - Arrow-function methods (for stable references): `subscribe = (listener: () => void): (() => void) => { this._listeners.add(listener); return () => this._listeners.delete(listener); }` and `getSnapshot = (): number => this._version`
   - Private `_notify()` method that increments `_version` and calls all listeners
   - Call `_notify()` at end of `register()`, `registerAsync()`, and `unregister()` — only when the state actually changed (skip if code-dedup early-return fires)

2. **Fix first-render timing** in `useEffectRegistry.ts:28-31`: Move `replaceEffectRegistry(registry)` out of `useEffect` and call it synchronously during render, immediately after `useMemo` creates the registry. This ensures the module singleton points to the provider registry before any child component renders. The async registration still happens in `useEffect`, but the registry object itself is available synchronously.

   ```typescript
   const registry = useMemo(() => new DynamicEffectRegistry(BUILT_INS), []);
   // Sync: make the module singleton point to this instance immediately
   replaceEffectRegistry(registry);
   ```

3. **Add** `useSyncExternalStore` subscription in `useEffectRegistry.ts` after the useMemo:
   ```typescript
   const _version = useSyncExternalStore(registry.subscribe, registry.getSnapshot);
   ```
   This forces `InnerProvider` (the only caller, `VideoEditorProvider.tsx:21`) to re-render when async registration completes. Since `InnerProvider` wraps all children, this propagation ensures the React tree sees updated state. Remotion compositions additionally re-render every frame via `useCurrentFrame()`, providing a second path to pick up changes.

### Step 2: Add EffectErrorBoundary with explicit fallback prop (`effects/EffectErrorBoundary.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `EffectErrorBoundary` class component with props `{ effectName: string; fallback: ReactNode; children: ReactNode }`. The `fallback` prop receives the content **before** effect wrapping — this is the key insight from the critique.
2. **On error:** `getDerivedStateFromError` sets `hasError: true`. `componentDidCatch` logs `[EffectErrorBoundary] "${effectName}" threw: ${error.message}`. `render()` returns `this.props.fallback` when errored, `this.props.children` otherwise.
3. **Reset** when `effectName` changes via `getDerivedStateFromError` returning `{ hasError: false }` when the key differs — or simply use a `key={effectName}` prop at the call site.

**Why fallback prop is needed:** In `wrapWithClipEffects`, `<Continuous>{wrapped}</Continuous>` is the effect wrapping clip content. If `Continuous` throws, an error boundary around it only sees the combined subtree. The boundary cannot extract the pre-wrapped `wrapped` content from `children`. By passing `wrapped` as `fallback` explicitly, the boundary can render the clip without the effect on error.

### Step 3: Wire EffectErrorBoundary into all effect rendering sites (`effects/index.tsx`, `compositions/EffectLayerSequence.tsx`, `components/EffectCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In `wrapWithClipEffects`** (`effects/index.tsx:114-170`): For each effect category, capture the pre-wrap content and wrap with boundary:
   ```typescript
   // Continuous effect (line ~122-137)
   if (continuous) {
     const beforeContinuous = wrapped;
     wrapped = (
       <EffectErrorBoundary effectName={clip.continuous!.type} fallback={beforeContinuous}>
         <Continuous durationInFrames={durationInFrames} intensity={clip.continuous?.intensity ?? 0.5} params={clip.continuous?.params}>
           {beforeContinuous}
         </Continuous>
       </EffectErrorBoundary>
     );
   }
   // Entrance effect (line ~139-152) — uses clip.entrance!.type
   // Exit effect (line ~154-167) — uses clip.exit!.type
   ```
   Each boundary receives the correct `effectName` for its category (`clip.continuous.type`, `clip.entrance.type`, `clip.exit.type` respectively).

2. **In `EffectLayerSequence.tsx:39-47`**: Wrap the `<Effect>` component with boundary, passing `<>{children}</>` as fallback:
   ```typescript
   const beforeEffect = <>{children}</>;
   return (
     <EffectErrorBoundary effectName={clip.continuous!.type} fallback={beforeEffect}>
       <Effect durationInFrames={durationInFrames} effectFrames={durationInFrames} intensity={clip.continuous.intensity ?? 0.5} params={clip.continuous.params}>
         {children}
       </Effect>
     </EffectErrorBoundary>
   );
   ```
   Note: `EffectLayerSequence` always passes `effectFrames={durationInFrames}` for continuous effects — this differing contract is preserved.

3. **In `EffectCreatorPanel.tsx:151-162`** (`makePreviewComposition`): Wrap the `<EffectComponent>` with boundary, passing `<PreviewRect>` as fallback.

### Step 4: Extend tests (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.test.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add test:** `subscribe` listener fires and `getSnapshot` increments when `register`/`registerAsync` is called.
2. **Add test:** `subscribe` listener does NOT fire when `register` is called with duplicate code (dedup early-return).
3. **Add test:** EffectErrorBoundary renders fallback when effect component throws at runtime. Create a component that throws on render, wrap in boundary with a `<div data-testid="fallback">` fallback, assert fallback renders.
4. **Existing tests** must continue to pass: built-in preference over collisions, compile error wrapping.

## Phase 2: Param Validation + Source Consolidation

### Step 5: Store parameterSchema in DynamicEffectRegistry (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the `dynamic` record type from `{ component: FC<EffectComponentProps>; code: string }` to `{ component: FC<EffectComponentProps>; code: string; schema?: ParameterSchema }`.
2. **Add** optional `schema?: ParameterSchema` parameter to `register(name, code, schema?)` and `registerAsync(name, code, schema?)`. Store it in the dynamic record.
3. **Add** `getSchema(name: string): ParameterSchema | undefined` method that returns `this.dynamic[this.normalizeName(name)]?.schema`.
4. **Update** `useEffectRegistry.ts` to pass `effect.parameterSchema` from `EffectResource` when calling `registry.registerAsync` for resource effects:
   ```typescript
   ...resources.map((effect) => registry.registerAsync(effect.id, effect.code, effect.parameterSchema))
   ```
   DB effects and drafts pass `undefined` for schema (they don't have it).

### Step 6: Add `validateAndCoerceParams` utility (`effects/validateParams.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `validateAndCoerceParams(params: Record<string, unknown> | undefined, schema: ParameterSchema | undefined): Record<string, unknown>` that:
   - Returns `params ?? {}` if `schema` is undefined or empty (fast path — built-in effects have no schema)
   - Starts with defaults from `getDefaultValues(schema)` (reuse the existing export from `ParameterControls.tsx:35`)
   - Overlays user-provided params, but **coerces** each value against its `ParameterDefinition`:
     - `number`: `typeof value === 'number' ? clamp(value, def.min, def.max) : defaultValue`
     - `boolean`: `typeof value === 'boolean' ? value : defaultValue`
     - `select`: `def.options?.some(o => o.value === value) ? value : defaultValue`
     - `color`: `typeof value === 'string' && /^#[0-9a-f]{3,8}$/i.test(value) ? value : defaultValue`
   - Returns the merged+coerced object
2. **Do not** export `getFallbackValue` from ParameterControls — use the existing `getDefaultValues` export as the base.

### Step 7: Apply validateAndCoerceParams at render-time (`effects/index.tsx`, `compositions/EffectLayerSequence.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In `wrapWithClipEffects`** (`effects/index.tsx`): Extract the registry reference once at the top via `const registry = getEffectRegistry()`. Before passing `params` to each effect, call `validateAndCoerceParams`:
   ```typescript
   // For continuous:
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.continuous?.params, registry.getSchema(clip.continuous!.type))}
   // For entrance:
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.entrance?.params, registry.getSchema(clip.entrance!.type))}
   // For exit:
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.exit?.params, registry.getSchema(clip.exit!.type))}
   ```
2. **In `EffectLayerSequence.tsx:40-45`**: Same pattern:
   ```typescript
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.continuous.params, getEffectRegistry().getSchema(clip.continuous.type))}
   ```
3. **Signature of `wrapWithClipEffects`** is unchanged — callers (`VisualClip.tsx:223`, `TextClip.tsx:45`) pass the same arguments.

### Step 8: Consolidate effect sources with explicit priority (`hooks/useEffectRegistry.ts`, `hooks/useEffects.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Change** the `Promise.all` in `useEffectRegistry.ts:39-47` to register sequentially by priority (drafts → DB → resources). Use chained awaits so later sources overwrite earlier ones:
   ```typescript
   void (async () => {
     // Lowest priority: drafts
     for (const [name, code] of drafts) {
       await registry.registerAsync(name, code);
     }
     // Medium priority: legacy DB effects
     if (db.length > 0) {
       console.warn('[EffectRegistry] %d effects loaded from legacy DB table — consider migrating to resources', db.length);
     }
     for (const effect of db) {
       await registry.registerAsync(effect.slug, effect.code);
     }
     // Highest priority: resource-based effects (with schema)
     for (const effect of resources) {
       await registry.registerAsync(effect.id, effect.code, effect.parameterSchema);
     }
   })();
   ```
2. **Add** `@deprecated` JSDoc to `useEffects.ts:6`:
   ```typescript
   /** @deprecated Legacy effects table — use resource-based effects via useEffectResources instead. */
   ```
3. **Note on `customEffects` persistence** (`serialize.ts:96-136`, `migrate.ts:165-186`, `timeline-data.ts:198-300`): The legacy `customEffects` field in timeline serialization is a broader migration concern. This plan fixes the runtime registry merging; the persistence migration is out of scope but acknowledged here. A future task should remove `customEffects` from the serialization pipeline once all effects are resource-based.

### Step 9: Align EffectCreatorPanel preview with timeline wrapping (`components/EffectCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Refactor** `makePreviewComposition` (`EffectCreatorPanel.tsx:139-164`) to use `wrapWithClipEffects` directly. Construct a synthetic `ResolvedTimelineClip` with the preview's settings:
   ```typescript
   function makePreviewComposition(
     EffectComponent: FC<EffectComponentProps> | null,
     category: EffectCategory,
     params: PreviewParams,
     effectParams: Record<string, unknown>,
     timelineFps: number,
     assetSrc?: string | null,
   ) {
     // Register the preview component temporarily in a local registry
     // so lookupEffect can find it — do NOT mutate the global registry
     const previewRegistry = new DynamicEffectRegistry({});
     // ... register synchronously if EffectComponent exists
   ```

2. **Avoid mutating the global registry.** Instead, since `wrapWithClipEffects` calls `lookupEffect` which reads the global registry, and we don't want to pollute it, take a different approach: **inline the wrapping logic** for the preview. Create a small `wrapPreviewEffect` function local to EffectCreatorPanel that mirrors `wrapWithClipEffects` wrapping order but uses the already-compiled `EffectComponent` directly:
   ```typescript
   function wrapPreviewEffect(
     content: ReactNode,
     EffectComponent: FC<EffectComponentProps>,
     category: EffectCategory,
     params: PreviewParams,
     effectParams: Record<string, unknown>,
     fps: number,
   ): ReactNode {
     const durationInFrames = Math.max(1, Math.round(params.durationSeconds * fps));
     const effectFrames = Math.max(1, Math.round(params.effectSeconds * fps));
     // Apply the effect in the same position that wrapWithClipEffects would
     if (category === 'continuous') {
       return <EffectComponent durationInFrames={durationInFrames} effectFrames={durationInFrames} intensity={params.intensity} params={effectParams}>{content}</EffectComponent>;
     }
     // entrance/exit: same prop shape as wrapWithClipEffects
     return <EffectComponent durationInFrames={durationInFrames} effectFrames={effectFrames} intensity={params.intensity} params={effectParams}>{content}</EffectComponent>;
   }
   ```
   This is faithful because it uses the same props shape as `wrapWithClipEffects` (`durationInFrames`, `effectFrames`, `intensity`, `params`) and applies the `effectFrames={durationInFrames}` pattern for continuous effects, matching `EffectLayerSequence.tsx:43`.

3. **Use timeline FPS for preview:** Replace `PREVIEW_FPS = 30` with a prop or context-derived `fps` value. The `EffectCreatorPanel` already renders inside the video editor context, so read `config.output.fps` from the timeline context (via `useVideoEditorRuntime()` or similar). If unavailable (panel opened standalone), fall back to 30. This ensures spring/frame-based animations match timeline timing.

4. **Keep** the `PREVIEW_SIZE = 320` for the Player viewport — this is intentional for the preview UI size and does not affect animation timing.

5. **Wrap** the preview effect in `<EffectErrorBoundary>` (already addressed in Step 3).

## Phase 3: Validation

### Step 10: Run existing tests
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `DynamicEffectRegistry.test.tsx` — must pass (built-in preference, compile error wrapping, new reactivity tests).
2. **Run** `useEffectResources.test.tsx` — must pass (dedup, grouping).
3. **Run** `VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx` — must pass (provider composition).
4. **Run** full video-editor test suite to catch regressions.

### Step 11: Manual smoke tests
**Scope:** Small (info only)
1. Open video editor, verify built-in effects still render on clips.
2. Create a custom effect via EffectCreatorPanel, verify preview matches timeline behavior.
3. Apply a custom effect to a clip, verify it renders in the timeline.
4. Create a custom effect with parameters, verify defaults fill in and wrong types are coerced.
5. Create a custom effect that throws at runtime, verify the clip still displays without the effect (graceful degradation via fallback).

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (reactive registry + first-render fix) — foundation for everything else.
2. Steps 2-3 (error boundary) — independent of param validation, can overlap with step 1.
3. Step 4 (tests) — validate steps 1-3 before proceeding.
4. Steps 5-7 (param validation) — depends on step 5 extending the registry from step 1.
5. Step 8 (source consolidation) — independent of param work.
6. Step 9 (preview alignment) — depends on steps 5-7 for validateAndCoerceParams.
7. Steps 10-11 — final validation.

## Validation Order
1. Run targeted tests after step 4.
2. Run full video-editor suite after step 8.
3. Manual smoke tests after step 9.

  "meta_commentary": "Key execution decisions the plan leaves to the executor:\n\n1. **useLayoutEffect over render-time mutation**: The plan says replaceEffectRegistry() in render (Step 1.2). The gate explicitly says useLayoutEffect is an acceptable safer alternative. USE useLayoutEffect \u2014 it runs synchronously before paint, closing the first-render gap without violating React purity. This satisfies the relaxed criterion: \"module singleton available before first child render.\"\n\n2. **batch() API design**: Add a simple `batch(fn: () => Promise<void>)` method to DynamicEffectRegistry that sets a `_batching = true` flag, awaits fn, then sets `_batching = false` and calls `_notify()` once. The `_notify()` method should early-return when `_batching` is true. This prevents O(n) re-renders during initialization.\n\n3. **validateParams layering**: The plan says reuse getDefaultValues from ParameterControls. The conventions flag correctly identifies this as a layering inversion (effects/ importing from components/). Instead, duplicate the ~15 lines of getFallbackValue logic directly in validateParams.ts. This is deliberate duplication to maintain layer boundaries \u2014 it's the right call.\n\n4. **Continuous effect contract divergence**: wrapWithClipEffects (index.tsx:128-136) does NOT pass effectFrames to continuous effects \u2014 only durationInFrames and intensity. EffectLayerSequence (line 42) passes effectFrames={durationInFrames}. Both are correct for their contexts. The preview's wrapPreviewEffect should use the EffectLayerSequence pattern for continuous (effectFrames=durationInFrames) since it's a standalone preview.\n\n5. **Preview FPS**: Keep hardcoded at 30. Do NOT try to thread fps from context \u2014 accepted tradeoff, would require plumbing changes to ClipPanel and DataProviderContext that are out of scope.\n\n6. **TypeScript**: registerAsync signature gains optional `schema?: ParameterSchema`. This is backward-compatible \u2014 all existing callers pass 2 args. Only resource registration passes the third arg.",

  "changes_summary": "Major revisions addressing 21 critique flags:\n\n**Architecture correction (FLAG-002, correctness-2, callers-1, all_locations-1/2, issue_hints-1, criteria_quality-3):** Rewrote the reactive registry approach to correctly model the provider-local \u2192 module-singleton \u2192 render-path chain. Moved `replaceEffectRegistry()` to synchronous render (fixing first-render gap). Added `useSyncExternalStore` in `useEffectRegistry` which triggers InnerProvider re-render, propagating to children. Remotion compositions additionally re-render every frame, providing a second update path.\n\n**Error boundary fix (FLAG-001, correctness-1, criteria_quality-2):** Added explicit `fallback` prop to EffectErrorBoundary. Each wrapping site passes the pre-wrapped content as fallback, so on error the boundary renders the clip without the effect. Detailed the wrapping pattern in Step 3 with correct `effectName` per category.\n\n**effectName typo fix (correctness-3):** Each boundary now uses the correct type (`clip.entrance.type`, `clip.exit.type`, `clip.continuous.type`).\n\n**Param validation upgraded (FLAG-003, issue_hints-2, callers-3, criteria_quality-1):** Replaced `fillDefaultParams` with `validateAndCoerceParams` that coerces types (number clamping, boolean check, select option validation, color regex). Uses existing `getDefaultValues` export rather than exposing `getFallbackValue`.\n\n**Preview fix (FLAG-004, correctness-4, issue_hints-3):** Abandoned global registry mutation approach. Instead, inline a `wrapPreviewEffect` local function that mirrors `wrapWithClipEffects` prop shapes. Added timeline FPS derivation from context instead of hardcoded PREVIEW_FPS.\n\n**Scope narrowing (scope-1, scope-2):** Removed CustomEffectEditor migration (dead code, no callers).   "recommendation": "PROCEED",
  "rationale": "Score improved 41% (30.5 \u2192 18.0) with 18 of 21 v1 flags resolved and a 71% plan delta. The remaining 11 flags cluster into 3 themes that are execution-level details, not architectural gaps:\n\n1. **Render purity (correctness-1/5, criteria_quality):** The `replaceEffectRegistry()` in render concern is a design tension, not a correctness bug. The assignment is idempotent (same useMemo instance), and React 18 tolerates idempotent external writes during render (this is how useSyncExternalStore-adjacent patterns work). The executor can alternatively use `useLayoutEffect` if they prefer purity \u2014 the success criterion should be relaxed from \"synchronous during render\" to \"module singleton available before first child render\" (which useLayoutEffect also satisfies).\n\n2. **Preview FPS source (FLAG-004, correctness-2, issue_hints, callers):** Four flags all identify the same gap: no context exposes FPS to EffectCreatorPanel. This is a minor threading issue \u2014 ClipPanel has timeline state access and can pass fps as a prop. The plan already specifies a 30fps fallback, which matches the typical timeline FPS. This is a \"should\" priority improvement, not a blocker.\n\n3. **Batch notifications (performance-1, all_locations):** O(n) re-renders during init is real but bounded (typically 5-20 effects) and React 18 batches async updates. The executor should add a simple `batch(fn)` wrapper or notify once after all registrations complete.\n\nThe core architecture is now correct: error boundary with explicit fallback prop, reactive registry via useSyncExternalStore propagating through InnerProvider, validateAndCoerceParams with type coercion, local wrapPreviewEffect avoiding global registry mutation, and sequential priority registration.",
  "signals_assessment": "Iteration 2. Weighted score trajectory: 30.5 \u2192 18.0 (41% improvement). Plan delta 71% \u2014 substantial revision that resolved 18 flags and correctly addressed all v1 architectural misunderstandings (error boundary fallback, render-path reactivity, param coercion, preview isolation). Remaining 11 open flags deduplicate to 3 clusters: render-purity tension (resolvable during execution), preview FPS threading (minor prop-passing gap), and batch notifications (straightforward performance optimization). No recurring critiques. Preflight passed. No overlap with escalated debt subsystems.",
  "warnings": [
    "Scope creep detected: the plan appears to be expanding beyond the original idea or recorded user notes.",
    "The 'replaceEffectRegistry in render' success criterion is over-specified \u2014 relax to 'module singleton available before first child render' so the executor can choose between render-time assignment or useLayoutEffect",
    "Preview FPS: the executor must either thread fps from ClipPanel as a prop or access useTimelineState directly \u2014 the plan's 'read from context' is not implementable as written",
    "Add a batch/suppress-notify mechanism to DynamicEffectRegistry to avoid O(n) provider re-renders during initialization \u2014 notify once after all registrations complete, not per-registration"
  ],
  "settled_decisions": [
    {
      "id": "SD-001",
      "decision": "EffectErrorBoundary takes an explicit fallback prop containing the pre-wrapped clip content",
      "rationale": "On error, the boundary renders the clip without the effect by rendering the fallback prop. This was validated in v1\u2192v2 revision and resolves the original FLAG-001."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-002",
      "decision": "Reactivity flows through useSyncExternalStore in useEffectRegistry, triggering InnerProvider re-render which propagates to all children",
      "rationale": "The module singleton pattern is preserved. Reactivity is achieved by subscribing to registry version changes, causing the provider (sole caller) to re-render its subtree. Remotion's useCurrentFrame provides a secondary update path."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-003",
      "decision": "validateAndCoerceParams replaces fillDefaultParams \u2014 it coerces wrong types, not just fills defaults",
      "rationale": "Uses existing getDefaultValues as base, adds per-type coercion (number clamping, boolean check, select validation, color regex). Does not export getFallbackValue."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-004",
      "decision": "Preview uses local wrapPreviewEffect function, never mutates the global registry",
      "rationale": "The preview mirrors wrapWithClipEffects prop shapes but uses the already-compiled EffectComponent directly, avoiding shared-state coupling."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-005",
      "decision": "CustomEffectEditor.tsx is dead code (no callers) \u2014 excluded from scope",
      "rationale": "Ripgrep confirmed no call sites. The plan does not migrate or modify it."
    }
  ],

      {
        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "video-editor-effects: EffectErrorBoundary fallback is underspecified; wrapping an effect component cannot recover raw clip content after a render throw unless the boundary also receives an explicit fallback node or equivalent split render path.",
        "resolution": "Major revisions addressing 21 critique flags:\n\n**Architecture correction (FLAG-002, correctness-2, callers-1, all_locations-1/2, issue_hints-1, criteria_quality-3):** Rewrote the reactive registry approach to correctly model the provider-local \u2192 module-singleton \u2192 render-path chain. Moved `replaceEffectRegistry()` to synchronous render (fixing first-render gap). Added `useSyncExternalStore` in `useEffectRegistry` which triggers InnerProvider re-render, propagating to children. Remotion compositions additionally re-render every frame, providing a second update path.\n\n**Error boundary fix (FLAG-001, correctness-1, criteria_quality-2):** Added explicit `fallback` prop to EffectErrorBoundary. Each wrapping site passes the pre-wrapped content as fallback, so on error the boundary renders the clip without the effect. Detailed the wrapping pattern in Step 3 with correct `effectName` per category.\n\n**effectName typo fix (correctness-3):** Each boundary now uses the correct type (`clip.entrance.type`, `clip.exit.type`, `clip.continuous.type`).\n\n**Param validation upgraded (FLAG-003, issue_hints-2, callers-3, criteria_quality-1):** Replaced `fillDefaultParams` with `validateAndCoerceParams` that coerces types (number clamping, boolean check, select option validation, color regex). Uses existing `getDefaultValues` export rather than exposing `getFallbackValue`.\n\n**Preview fix (FLAG-004, correctness-4, issue_hints-3):** Abandoned global registry mutation approach. Instead, inline a `wrapPreviewEffect` local function that mirrors `wrapWithClipEffects` prop shapes. Added timeline FPS derivation from context instead of hardcoded PREVIEW_FPS.\n\n**Scope narrowing (scope-1, scope-2):** Removed CustomEffectEditor migration (dead code, no callers). Acknowledged customEffects persistence as out of scope. Removed scope creep around legacy persistence migration."

        Settled decisions (verify the executor implemented these correctly):
- SD-001: EffectErrorBoundary takes an explicit fallback prop containing the pre-wrapped clip content (On error, the boundary renders the clip without the effect by rendering the fallback prop. This was validated in v1→v2 revision and resolves the original FLAG-001.)
- SD-002: Reactivity flows through useSyncExternalStore in useEffectRegistry, triggering InnerProvider re-render which propagates to all children (The module singleton pattern is preserved. Reactivity is achieved by subscribing to registry version changes, causing the provider (sole caller) to re-render its subtree. Remotion's useCurrentFrame provides a secondary update path.)
- SD-003: validateAndCoerceParams replaces fillDefaultParams — it coerces wrong types, not just fills defaults (Uses existing getDefaultValues as base, adds per-type coercion (number clamping, boolean check, select validation, color regex). Does not export getFallbackValue.)
- SD-004: Preview uses local wrapPreviewEffect function, never mutates the global registry (The preview mirrors wrapWithClipEffects prop shapes but uses the already-compiled EffectComponent directly, avoiding shared-state coupling.)
- SD-005: CustomEffectEditor.tsx is dead code (no callers) — excluded from scope (Ripgrep confirmed no call sites. The plan does not migrate or modify it.)


        A researcher recommended you consider these points in executing the task:

        - [CRITICAL] replaceEffectRegistry() during render is a side effect that violates React rules
  Step 1.2 proposes moving `replaceEffectRegistry(registry)` out of useEffect and calling it synchronously during render. This mutates module-level state (`let effectRegistry`) during render, which is a side effect. React requires render functions to be pure — side effects must go in useEffect, event handlers, or useMemo initializers. While the codebase doesn't currently use StrictMode, this would break under StrictMode (double-invocation) and violates React's concurrent rendering contract. The current useEffect placement is the correct React pattern. Instead, the plan should use `useMemo` to create AND replace the registry atomically: `const registry = useMemo(() => { const r = new DynamicEffectRegistry(BUILT_INS); replaceEffectRegistry(r); return r; }, [])` — this is idempotent and safe under double-render since it only runs once due to the empty deps.
  Source: src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useEffectRegistry.ts:28-31, React docs on purity of render
- [IMPORTANT] EffectCreatorPanel has NO access to timeline FPS from context — plan's Step 9.3 is unimplementable as described
  Step 9.3 says 'read config.output.fps from the timeline context (via useVideoEditorRuntime() or similar)'. But useVideoEditorRuntime() only provides {provider, timelineId, userId, timelineName} — no FPS. The timeline config (including output.fps) lives in useTimelineState(), which is called in InnerProvider but not exposed to EffectCreatorPanel. EffectCreatorPanel is rendered inside ClipPanel which also doesn't access FPS. To get FPS, the plan would need to either: (a) add an fps prop to EffectCreatorPanel passed down from a parent that has config access, (b) create a new context/hook for config access, or (c) keep PREVIEW_FPS=30 as a reasonable default since the actual timeline FPS is typically 30 anyway. Option (c) is simplest and the divergence is cosmetic.
  Source: src/tools/video-editor/contexts/DataProviderContext.tsx:4-9, EffectCreatorPanel.tsx imports
- [IMPORTANT] Sequential async registration (Step 8) will fire _notify() N times, causing N re-renders of InnerProvider
  Step 8 changes from Promise.all to sequential await loops. With the reactive registry from Step 1, each `registerAsync` call fires `_notify()` which increments the version and triggers useSyncExternalStore to re-render InnerProvider. For a user with 20 effects (drafts + DB + resources), this means 20 re-renders during initialization instead of the current single batch. The plan should either: (a) add a `batch` mode that suppresses notifications until all registrations complete, then fires once, or (b) keep Promise.all for parallel registration and do a priority-enforcement pass afterward (register all, then for collisions, re-register the higher-priority one to overwrite).
  Source: DynamicEffectRegistry.ts register/registerAsync, useEffectRegistry.ts:39-47
- [IMPORTANT] makePreviewComposition doesn't pass effectFrames=durationInFrames for continuous — plan's wrapPreviewEffect correctly identifies this but current code always passes effectFrames
  The current makePreviewComposition (line 153-154) always passes both durationInFrames AND effectFrames regardless of effect category. For continuous effects, the timeline (EffectLayerSequence.tsx:42) passes effectFrames=durationInFrames. The plan's wrapPreviewEffect correctly handles this. However, the plan doesn't note that the current preview ALSO always passes effectFrames derived from effectSeconds (line 146), which for continuous effects should be durationInFrames instead. This is a real divergence the plan should fix — but the plan's Step 9.2 code sample already does this correctly for the continuous case. Just noting this is a genuine existing bug the plan silently fixes.
  Source: EffectCreatorPanel.tsx:145-154, EffectLayerSequence.tsx:42
- [IMPORTANT] _notify() will fire even when registerAsync early-returns due to code dedup
  Step 1.1 says 'Call _notify() at end of register(), registerAsync(), and unregister() — only when the state actually changed (skip if code-dedup early-return fires)'. This is correctly noted but needs careful implementation. The current code has early returns at lines 15 and 22: `if (this.dynamic[normalized]?.code === code) return`. The _notify() call must be placed AFTER these guards, not at the very end of the method. The plan says this correctly in prose but the implementation must match — easy to get wrong if _notify is placed in a finally block or similar.
  Source: DynamicEffectRegistry.ts:15,22
- [MINOR] Plan creates validateParams.ts but codebase convention puts utilities in effects/ or lib/
  Step 6 creates `effects/validateParams.ts`. Other effect utilities live in `effects/` (compileEffect.ts, effect-store.ts, DynamicEffectRegistry.ts) so this placement is correct. Minor note: the file imports getDefaultValues from `components/ParameterControls.tsx`, creating a dependency from effects/ to components/ — this is a slight inversion since effects/ is lower-level. Consider moving getDefaultValues to effects/ or a shared location if this bothers the team.
  Source: File organization in src/tools/video-editor/effects/
- [MINOR] Plan doesn't mention updating the Continuous effect prop types to include effectFrames
  wrapWithClipEffects passes Continuous effects with {durationInFrames, intensity, params} but EffectLayerSequence passes {durationInFrames, effectFrames, intensity, params}. The plan's validateAndCoerceParams integration (Step 7) adds params validation to both paths but doesn't address this prop inconsistency between the two continuous rendering paths. This is a pre-existing issue, not introduced by the plan, but worth noting since the plan touches both sites.
  Source: effects/index.tsx:129-131 vs compositions/EffectLayerSequence.tsx:42

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.
        - The decisions listed above were settled at the gate stage. Verify that the executor implemented each settled decision correctly. Flag deviations from these decisions, but do not question the decisions themselves.
        - The research considerations above are based on current documentation searches. Any item marked CRITICAL or IMPORTANT should be flagged if the plan doesn't address it.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app

        Idea:
Fix all 5 architectural concerns in the video-editor effect system: (1) Three effect sources merged silently — make migration path explicit or deprecate legacy DB path, (2) Global singleton registry — make reactive so components always use current registry state, (3) No runtime error boundary around effects — add safety net for buggy/AI-generated custom effects, (4) EffectCreatorPanel preview divergence — make preview composition more faithful to actual timeline rendering, (5) Untyped params — add runtime validation or default-filling from parameterSchema at apply-time. Key files: effects/index.tsx, DynamicEffectRegistry.ts, useEffectRegistry.ts, VisualClip.tsx, TextClip.tsx, EffectLayerSequence.tsx, EffectCreatorPanel.tsx, ParameterControls.tsx, types/index.ts

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Fix 5 Architectural Gaps in Video-Editor Effect System

## Overview

The video-editor effect system has five architectural gaps: (1) three effect sources merge silently with no dedup/priority, (2) the registry is non-reactive so components don't re-render when async registration completes, (3) no runtime error boundary for custom effects, (4) EffectCreatorPanel preview diverges from timeline wrapping pipeline, and (5) effect params are unvalidated `Record<string, unknown>` at apply-time.

**Architecture understanding:** `useEffectRegistry` (called in `VideoEditorProvider.tsx:21`) creates a provider-local `DynamicEffectRegistry` via `useMemo()` and mirrors it into a module-level singleton via `replaceEffectRegistry()` inside `useEffect()`. All render-time lookups (`lookupEffect`, `wrapWithClipEffects`, `EffectLayerSequence`) read from `getEffectRegistry()` — the module singleton. Remotion compositions re-render every frame via `useCurrentFrame()`, so once the module singleton is updated, clips pick up changes on the next frame. The core timing issue is that `replaceEffectRegistry()` runs in `useEffect()` (post-render), so the first render may hit a stale default registry.

**Scope:** `CustomEffectEditor.tsx` has no callers in the codebase (dead code) — excluded from this plan. The `customEffects` field in serialization/migration (`serialize.ts`, `migrate.ts`, `timeline-data.ts`) is a broader persistence concern outside the scope of these 5 architectural fixes.

## Phase 1: Foundation — Reactive Registry + Error Boundary

### Step 1: Make DynamicEffectRegistry reactive and fix first-render timing (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts`, `hooks/useEffectRegistry.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** to `DynamicEffectRegistry` class (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts`):
   - Private `_version: number = 0` and `_listeners: Set<() => void> = new Set()`
   - Arrow-function methods (for stable references): `subscribe = (listener: () => void): (() => void) => { this._listeners.add(listener); return () => this._listeners.delete(listener); }` and `getSnapshot = (): number => this._version`
   - Private `_notify()` method that increments `_version` and calls all listeners
   - Call `_notify()` at end of `register()`, `registerAsync()`, and `unregister()` — only when the state actually changed (skip if code-dedup early-return fires)

2. **Fix first-render timing** in `useEffectRegistry.ts:28-31`: Move `replaceEffectRegistry(registry)` out of `useEffect` and call it synchronously during render, immediately after `useMemo` creates the registry. This ensures the module singleton points to the provider registry before any child component renders. The async registration still happens in `useEffect`, but the registry object itself is available synchronously.

   ```typescript
   const registry = useMemo(() => new DynamicEffectRegistry(BUILT_INS), []);
   // Sync: make the module singleton point to this instance immediately
   replaceEffectRegistry(registry);
   ```

3. **Add** `useSyncExternalStore` subscription in `useEffectRegistry.ts` after the useMemo:
   ```typescript
   const _version = useSyncExternalStore(registry.subscribe, registry.getSnapshot);
   ```
   This forces `InnerProvider` (the only caller, `VideoEditorProvider.tsx:21`) to re-render when async registration completes. Since `InnerProvider` wraps all children, this propagation ensures the React tree sees updated state. Remotion compositions additionally re-render every frame via `useCurrentFrame()`, providing a second path to pick up changes.

### Step 2: Add EffectErrorBoundary with explicit fallback prop (`effects/EffectErrorBoundary.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `EffectErrorBoundary` class component with props `{ effectName: string; fallback: ReactNode; children: ReactNode }`. The `fallback` prop receives the content **before** effect wrapping — this is the key insight from the critique.
2. **On error:** `getDerivedStateFromError` sets `hasError: true`. `componentDidCatch` logs `[EffectErrorBoundary] "${effectName}" threw: ${error.message}`. `render()` returns `this.props.fallback` when errored, `this.props.children` otherwise.
3. **Reset** when `effectName` changes via `getDerivedStateFromError` returning `{ hasError: false }` when the key differs — or simply use a `key={effectName}` prop at the call site.

**Why fallback prop is needed:** In `wrapWithClipEffects`, `<Continuous>{wrapped}</Continuous>` is the effect wrapping clip content. If `Continuous` throws, an error boundary around it only sees the combined subtree. The boundary cannot extract the pre-wrapped `wrapped` content from `children`. By passing `wrapped` as `fallback` explicitly, the boundary can render the clip without the effect on error.

### Step 3: Wire EffectErrorBoundary into all effect rendering sites (`effects/index.tsx`, `compositions/EffectLayerSequence.tsx`, `components/EffectCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In `wrapWithClipEffects`** (`effects/index.tsx:114-170`): For each effect category, capture the pre-wrap content and wrap with boundary:
   ```typescript
   // Continuous effect (line ~122-137)
   if (continuous) {
     const beforeContinuous = wrapped;
     wrapped = (
       <EffectErrorBoundary effectName={clip.continuous!.type} fallback={beforeContinuous}>
         <Continuous durationInFrames={durationInFrames} intensity={clip.continuous?.intensity ?? 0.5} params={clip.continuous?.params}>
           {beforeContinuous}
         </Continuous>
       </EffectErrorBoundary>
     );
   }
   // Entrance effect (line ~139-152) — uses clip.entrance!.type
   // Exit effect (line ~154-167) — uses clip.exit!.type
   ```
   Each boundary receives the correct `effectName` for its category (`clip.continuous.type`, `clip.entrance.type`, `clip.exit.type` respectively).

2. **In `EffectLayerSequence.tsx:39-47`**: Wrap the `<Effect>` component with boundary, passing `<>{children}</>` as fallback:
   ```typescript
   const beforeEffect = <>{children}</>;
   return (
     <EffectErrorBoundary effectName={clip.continuous!.type} fallback={beforeEffect}>
       <Effect durationInFrames={durationInFrames} effectFrames={durationInFrames} intensity={clip.continuous.intensity ?? 0.5} params={clip.continuous.params}>
         {children}
       </Effect>
     </EffectErrorBoundary>
   );
   ```
   Note: `EffectLayerSequence` always passes `effectFrames={durationInFrames}` for continuous effects — this differing contract is preserved.

3. **In `EffectCreatorPanel.tsx:151-162`** (`makePreviewComposition`): Wrap the `<EffectComponent>` with boundary, passing `<PreviewRect>` as fallback.

### Step 4: Extend tests (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.test.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add test:** `subscribe` listener fires and `getSnapshot` increments when `register`/`registerAsync` is called.
2. **Add test:** `subscribe` listener does NOT fire when `register` is called with duplicate code (dedup early-return).
3. **Add test:** EffectErrorBoundary renders fallback when effect component throws at runtime. Create a component that throws on render, wrap in boundary with a `<div data-testid="fallback">` fallback, assert fallback renders.
4. **Existing tests** must continue to pass: built-in preference over collisions, compile error wrapping.

## Phase 2: Param Validation + Source Consolidation

### Step 5: Store parameterSchema in DynamicEffectRegistry (`effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the `dynamic` record type from `{ component: FC<EffectComponentProps>; code: string }` to `{ component: FC<EffectComponentProps>; code: string; schema?: ParameterSchema }`.
2. **Add** optional `schema?: ParameterSchema` parameter to `register(name, code, schema?)` and `registerAsync(name, code, schema?)`. Store it in the dynamic record.
3. **Add** `getSchema(name: string): ParameterSchema | undefined` method that returns `this.dynamic[this.normalizeName(name)]?.schema`.
4. **Update** `useEffectRegistry.ts` to pass `effect.parameterSchema` from `EffectResource` when calling `registry.registerAsync` for resource effects:
   ```typescript
   ...resources.map((effect) => registry.registerAsync(effect.id, effect.code, effect.parameterSchema))
   ```
   DB effects and drafts pass `undefined` for schema (they don't have it).

### Step 6: Add `validateAndCoerceParams` utility (`effects/validateParams.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `validateAndCoerceParams(params: Record<string, unknown> | undefined, schema: ParameterSchema | undefined): Record<string, unknown>` that:
   - Returns `params ?? {}` if `schema` is undefined or empty (fast path — built-in effects have no schema)
   - Starts with defaults from `getDefaultValues(schema)` (reuse the existing export from `ParameterControls.tsx:35`)
   - Overlays user-provided params, but **coerces** each value against its `ParameterDefinition`:
     - `number`: `typeof value === 'number' ? clamp(value, def.min, def.max) : defaultValue`
     - `boolean`: `typeof value === 'boolean' ? value : defaultValue`
     - `select`: `def.options?.some(o => o.value === value) ? value : defaultValue`
     - `color`: `typeof value === 'string' && /^#[0-9a-f]{3,8}$/i.test(value) ? value : defaultValue`
   - Returns the merged+coerced object
2. **Do not** export `getFallbackValue` from ParameterControls — use the existing `getDefaultValues` export as the base.

### Step 7: Apply validateAndCoerceParams at render-time (`effects/index.tsx`, `compositions/EffectLayerSequence.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In `wrapWithClipEffects`** (`effects/index.tsx`): Extract the registry reference once at the top via `const registry = getEffectRegistry()`. Before passing `params` to each effect, call `validateAndCoerceParams`:
   ```typescript
   // For continuous:
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.continuous?.params, registry.getSchema(clip.continuous!.type))}
   // For entrance:
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.entrance?.params, registry.getSchema(clip.entrance!.type))}
   // For exit:
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.exit?.params, registry.getSchema(clip.exit!.type))}
   ```
2. **In `EffectLayerSequence.tsx:40-45`**: Same pattern:
   ```typescript
   params={validateAndCoerceParams(clip.continuous.params, getEffectRegistry().getSchema(clip.continuous.type))}
   ```
3. **Signature of `wrapWithClipEffects`** is unchanged — callers (`VisualClip.tsx:223`, `TextClip.tsx:45`) pass the same arguments.

### Step 8: Consolidate effect sources with explicit priority (`hooks/useEffectRegistry.ts`, `hooks/useEffects.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Change** the `Promise.all` in `useEffectRegistry.ts:39-47` to register sequentially by priority (drafts → DB → resources). Use chained awaits so later sources overwrite earlier ones:
   ```typescript
   void (async () => {
     // Lowest priority: drafts
     for (const [name, code] of drafts) {
       await registry.registerAsync(name, code);
     }
     // Medium priority: legacy DB effects
     if (db.length > 0) {
       console.warn('[EffectRegistry] %d effects loaded from legacy DB table — consider migrating to resources', db.length);
     }
     for (const effect of db) {
       await registry.registerAsync(effect.slug, effect.code);
     }
     // Highest priority: resource-based effects (with schema)
     for (const effect of resources) {
       await registry.registerAsync(effect.id, effect.code, effect.parameterSchema);
     }
   })();
   ```
2. **Add** `@deprecated` JSDoc to `useEffects.ts:6`:
   ```typescript
   /** @deprecated Legacy effects table — use resource-based effects via useEffectResources instead. */
   ```
3. **Note on `customEffects` persistence** (`serialize.ts:96-136`, `migrate.ts:165-186`, `timeline-data.ts:198-300`): The legacy `customEffects` field in timeline serialization is a broader migration concern. This plan fixes the runtime registry merging; the persistence migration is out of scope but acknowledged here. A future task should remove `customEffects` from the serialization pipeline once all effects are resource-based.

### Step 9: Align EffectCreatorPanel preview with timeline wrapping (`components/EffectCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Refactor** `makePreviewComposition` (`EffectCreatorPanel.tsx:139-164`) to use `wrapWithClipEffects` directly. Construct a synthetic `ResolvedTimelineClip` with the preview's settings:
   ```typescript
   function makePreviewComposition(
     EffectComponent: FC<EffectComponentProps> | null,
     category: EffectCategory,
     params: PreviewParams,
     effectParams: Record<string, unknown>,
     timelineFps: number,
     assetSrc?: string | null,
   ) {
     // Register the preview component temporarily in a local registry
     // so lookupEffect can find it — do NOT mutate the global registry
     const previewRegistry = new DynamicEffectRegistry({});
     // ... register synchronously if EffectComponent exists
   ```

2. **Avoid mutating the global registry.** Instead, since `wrapWithClipEffects` calls `lookupEffect` which reads the global registry, and we don't want to pollute it, take a different approach: **inline the wrapping logic** for the preview. Create a small `wrapPreviewEffect` function local to EffectCreatorPanel that mirrors `wrapWithClipEffects` wrapping order but uses the already-compiled `EffectComponent` directly:
   ```typescript
   function wrapPreviewEffect(
     content: ReactNode,
     EffectComponent: FC<EffectComponentProps>,
     category: EffectCategory,
     params: PreviewParams,
     effectParams: Record<string, unknown>,
     fps: number,
   ): ReactNode {
     const durationInFrames = Math.max(1, Math.round(params.durationSeconds * fps));
     const effectFrames = Math.max(1, Math.round(params.effectSeconds * fps));
     // Apply the effect in the same position that wrapWithClipEffects would
     if (category === 'continuous') {
       return <EffectComponent durationInFrames={durationInFrames} effectFrames={durationInFrames} intensity={params.intensity} params={effectParams}>{content}</EffectComponent>;
     }
     // entrance/exit: same prop shape as wrapWithClipEffects
     return <EffectComponent durationInFrames={durationInFrames} effectFrames={effectFrames} intensity={params.intensity} params={effectParams}>{content}</EffectComponent>;
   }
   ```
   This is faithful because it uses the same props shape as `wrapWithClipEffects` (`durationInFrames`, `effectFrames`, `intensity`, `params`) and applies the `effectFrames={durationInFrames}` pattern for continuous effects, matching `EffectLayerSequence.tsx:43`.

3. **Use timeline FPS for preview:** Replace `PREVIEW_FPS = 30` with a prop or context-derived `fps` value. The `EffectCreatorPanel` already renders inside the video editor context, so read `config.output.fps` from the timeline context (via `useVideoEditorRuntime()` or similar). If unavailable (panel opened standalone), fall back to 30. This ensures spring/frame-based animations match timeline timing.

4. **Keep** the `PREVIEW_SIZE = 320` for the Player viewport — this is intentional for the preview UI size and does not affect animation timing.

5. **Wrap** the preview effect in `<EffectErrorBoundary>` (already addressed in Step 3).

## Phase 3: Validation

### Step 10: Run existing tests
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** `DynamicEffectRegistry.test.tsx` — must pass (built-in preference, compile error wrapping, new reactivity tests).
2. **Run** `useEffectResources.test.tsx` — must pass (dedup, grouping).
3. **Run** `VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx` — must pass (provider composition).
4. **Run** full video-editor test suite to catch regressions.

### Step 11: Manual smoke tests
**Scope:** Small (info only)
1. Open video editor, verify built-in effects still render on clips.
2. Create a custom effect via EffectCreatorPanel, verify preview matches timeline behavior.
3. Apply a custom effect to a clip, verify it renders in the timeline.
4. Create a custom effect with parameters, verify defaults fill in and wrong types are coerced.
5. Create a custom effect that throws at runtime, verify the clip still displays without the effect (graceful degradation via fallback).

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (reactive registry + first-render fix) — foundation for everything else.
2. Steps 2-3 (error boundary) — independent of param validation, can overlap with step 1.
3. Step 4 (tests) — validate steps 1-3 before proceeding.
4. Steps 5-7 (param validation) — depends on step 5 extending the registry from step 1.
5. Step 8 (source consolidation) — independent of param work.
6. Step 9 (preview alignment) — depends on steps 5-7 for validateAndCoerceParams.
7. Steps 10-11 — final validation.

## Validation Order
1. Run targeted tests after step 4.
2. Run full video-editor suite after step 8.
3. Manual smoke tests after step 9.

  "meta_commentary": "Key execution decisions the plan leaves to the executor:\n\n1. **useLayoutEffect over render-time mutation**: The plan says replaceEffectRegistry() in render (Step 1.2). The gate explicitly says useLayoutEffect is an acceptable safer alternative. USE useLayoutEffect \u2014 it runs synchronously before paint, closing the first-render gap without violating React purity. This satisfies the relaxed criterion: \"module singleton available before first child render.\"\n\n2. **batch() API design**: Add a simple `batch(fn: () => Promise<void>)` method to DynamicEffectRegistry that sets a `_batching = true` flag, awaits fn, then sets `_batching = false` and calls `_notify()` once. The `_notify()` method should early-return when `_batching` is true. This prevents O(n) re-renders during initialization.\n\n3. **validateParams layering**: The plan says reuse getDefaultValues from ParameterControls. The conventions flag correctly identifies this as a layering inversion (effects/ importing from components/). Instead, duplicate the ~15 lines of getFallbackValue logic directly in validateParams.ts. This is deliberate duplication to maintain layer boundaries \u2014 it's the right call.\n\n4. **Continuous effect contract divergence**: wrapWithClipEffects (index.tsx:128-136) does NOT pass effectFrames to continuous effects \u2014 only durationInFrames and intensity. EffectLayerSequence (line 42) passes effectFrames={durationInFrames}. Both are correct for their contexts. The preview's wrapPreviewEffect should use the EffectLayerSequence pattern for continuous (effectFrames=durationInFrames) since it's a standalone preview.\n\n5. **Preview FPS**: Keep hardcoded at 30. Do NOT try to thread fps from context \u2014 accepted tradeoff, would require plumbing changes to ClipPanel and DataProviderContext that are out of scope.\n\n6. **TypeScript**: registerAsync signature gains optional `schema?: ParameterSchema`. This is backward-compatible \u2014 all existing callers pass 2 args. Only resource registration passes the third arg.",

        Plan metadata:
        {
  "version": 2,
  "timestamp": "2026-04-06T00:49:25Z",
  "hash": "sha256:5e4ee25358e9217188e52b3e3bc9a038e3d0a33d5d78bcf58877c6a5e0795232",
  "changes_summary": "Major revisions addressing 21 critique flags:\n\n**Architecture correction (FLAG-002, correctness-2, callers-1, all_locations-1/2, issue_hints-1, criteria_quality-3):** Rewrote the reactive registry approach to correctly model the provider-local \u2192 module-singleton \u2192 render-path chain. Moved `replaceEffectRegistry()` to synchronous render (fixing first-render gap). Added `useSyncExternalStore` in `useEffectRegistry` which triggers InnerProvider re-render, propagating to children. Remotion compositions additionally re-render every frame, providing a second update path.\n\n**Error boundary fix (FLAG-001, correctness-1, criteria_quality-2):** Added explicit `fallback` prop to EffectErrorBoundary. Each wrapping site passes the pre-wrapped content as fallback, so on error the boundary renders the clip without the effect. Detailed the wrapping pattern in Step 3 with correct `effectName` per category.\n\n**effectName typo fix (correctness-3):** Each boundary now uses the correct type (`clip.entrance.type`, `clip.exit.type`, `clip.continuous.type`).\n\n**Param validation upgraded (FLAG-003, issue_hints-2, callers-3, criteria_quality-1):** Replaced `fillDefaultParams` with `validateAndCoerceParams` that coerces types (number clamping, boolean check, select option validation, color regex). Uses existing `getDefaultValues` export rather than exposing `getFallbackValue`.\n\n**Preview fix (FLAG-004, correctness-4, issue_hints-3):** Abandoned global registry mutation approach. Instead, inline a `wrapPreviewEffect` local function that mirrors `wrapWithClipEffects` prop shapes. Added timeline FPS derivation from context instead of hardcoded PREVIEW_FPS.\n\n**Scope narrowing (scope-1, scope-2):** Removed CustomEffectEditor migration (dead code, no callers). Acknowledged customEffects persistence as out of scope. Removed scope creep around legacy persistence migration.",

  "questions": [
    "The preview in EffectCreatorPanel currently only shows one effect category at a time (entrance OR continuous OR exit). Should the aligned preview show all three layers like the timeline does, or keep showing just the single category being edited? (Assumed: keep single category since the panel edits one effect at a time.)"
  ],

    {
      "criterion": "All three existing test files pass: DynamicEffectRegistry.test.tsx, useEffectResources.test.tsx, VideoEditorProvider.test.tsx",
      "priority": "must"

      "criterion": "DynamicEffectRegistry exposes subscribe() and getSnapshot() as stable arrow-function methods compatible with useSyncExternalStore",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "replaceEffectRegistry() is called synchronously during render in useEffectRegistry (not inside useEffect), so the module singleton is available before first child render",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "useSyncExternalStore in useEffectRegistry triggers InnerProvider re-render when async registration completes, verified by a test that registers an effect and asserts the component tree updates",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "EffectErrorBoundary accepts a fallback prop (the pre-wrapped clip content) and renders it on error instead of the errored effect subtree",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "wrapWithClipEffects wraps each effect category (entrance, exit, continuous) in EffectErrorBoundary with the correct effectName and the pre-wrap content as fallback",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "EffectLayerSequence wraps its Effect rendering in EffectErrorBoundary with children as fallback",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "validateAndCoerceParams utility exists, fills missing params from schema defaults, and coerces wrong-type values (number clamping, boolean check, select option validation, color regex)",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "wrapWithClipEffects and EffectLayerSequence call validateAndCoerceParams before passing params to effect components",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "DynamicEffectRegistry stores and retrieves parameterSchema alongside effect components via register/registerAsync/getSchema",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "useEffectRegistry passes parameterSchema from EffectResource when registering resource effects",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Built-in effects still take priority over dynamic effects with the same name (existing invariant preserved)",
      "priority": "must"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Resource effects take priority over legacy DB effects when names collide (sequential registration order: drafts \u2192 DB \u2192 resources)",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Legacy useEffects.ts has @deprecated JSDoc annotation",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "EffectCreatorPanel preview uses wrapPreviewEffect that mirrors wrapWithClipEffects prop shapes (including effectFrames=durationInFrames for continuous)",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "EffectCreatorPanel reads timeline FPS from context instead of hardcoded PREVIEW_FPS (with 30fps fallback)",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "No changes to ClipEntrance/ClipExit/ClipContinuous types (params stays Record<string, unknown>)",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "New files stay under ~150 lines each (EffectErrorBoundary, validateParams)",
      "priority": "should"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Manual smoke test: custom effect with runtime throw degrades gracefully \u2014 clip renders without effect",
      "priority": "info"
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Manual smoke test: custom effect with wrong-type parameters gets coerced to valid values",
      "priority": "info"
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "CustomEffectEditor.tsx is dead code (no callers found) \u2014 excluded from migration scope.",
    "The customEffects field in serialization/migration is a broader persistence concern outside this plan's scope.",
    "Timeline FPS defaults to 30 (same as PREVIEW_FPS), but we'll read from context for correctness.",
    "Moving replaceEffectRegistry() to synchronous render is safe because it only sets a module-level variable (no side effects beyond assignment).",
    "Remotion compositions re-render every frame via useCurrentFrame(), providing natural reactivity for effect lookups.",
    "The preview panel renders inside the video editor context tree, so timeline config (including FPS) is accessible.",
    "Draft effects (localStorage) are the lowest priority source \u2014 they're for local testing before save and should be overwritten by any persisted source."

  "recommendation": "PROCEED",
  "rationale": "Score improved 41% (30.5 \u2192 18.0) with 18 of 21 v1 flags resolved and a 71% plan delta. The remaining 11 flags cluster into 3 themes that are execution-level details, not architectural gaps:\n\n1. **Render purity (correctness-1/5, criteria_quality):** The `replaceEffectRegistry()` in render concern is a design tension, not a correctness bug. The assignment is idempotent (same useMemo instance), and React 18 tolerates idempotent external writes during render (this is how useSyncExternalStore-adjacent patterns work). The executor can alternatively use `useLayoutEffect` if they prefer purity \u2014 the success criterion should be relaxed from \"synchronous during render\" to \"module singleton available before first child render\" (which useLayoutEffect also satisfies).\n\n2. **Preview FPS source (FLAG-004, correctness-2, issue_hints, callers):** Four flags all identify the same gap: no context exposes FPS to EffectCreatorPanel. This is a minor threading issue \u2014 ClipPanel has timeline state access and can pass fps as a prop. The plan already specifies a 30fps fallback, which matches the typical timeline FPS. This is a \"should\" priority improvement, not a blocker.\n\n3. **Batch notifications (performance-1, all_locations):** O(n) re-renders during init is real but bounded (typically 5-20 effects) and React 18 batches async updates. The executor should add a simple `batch(fn)` wrapper or notify once after all registrations complete.\n\nThe core architecture is now correct: error boundary with explicit fallback prop, reactive registry via useSyncExternalStore propagating through InnerProvider, validateAndCoerceParams with type coercion, local wrapPreviewEffect avoiding global registry mutation, and sequential priority registration.",
  "signals_assessment": "Iteration 2. Weighted score trajectory: 30.5 \u2192 18.0 (41% improvement). Plan delta 71% \u2014 substantial revision that resolved 18 flags and correctly addressed all v1 architectural misunderstandings (error boundary fallback, render-path reactivity, param coercion, preview isolation). Remaining 11 open flags deduplicate to 3 clusters: render-purity tension (resolvable during execution), preview FPS threading (minor prop-passing gap), and batch notifications (straightforward performance optimization). No recurring critiques. Preflight passed. No overlap with escalated debt subsystems.",
  "warnings": [
    "Scope creep detected: the plan appears to be expanding beyond the original idea or recorded user notes.",
    "The 'replaceEffectRegistry in render' success criterion is over-specified \u2014 relax to 'module singleton available before first child render' so the executor can choose between render-time assignment or useLayoutEffect",
    "Preview FPS: the executor must either thread fps from ClipPanel as a prop or access useTimelineState directly \u2014 the plan's 'read from context' is not implementable as written",
    "Add a batch/suppress-notify mechanism to DynamicEffectRegistry to avoid O(n) provider re-renders during initialization \u2014 notify once after all registrations complete, not per-registration"
  ],
  "settled_decisions": [
    {
      "id": "SD-001",
      "decision": "EffectErrorBoundary takes an explicit fallback prop containing the pre-wrapped clip content",
      "rationale": "On error, the boundary renders the clip without the effect by rendering the fallback prop. This was validated in v1\u2192v2 revision and resolves the original FLAG-001."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-002",
      "decision": "Reactivity flows through useSyncExternalStore in useEffectRegistry, triggering InnerProvider re-render which propagates to all children",
      "rationale": "The module singleton pattern is preserved. Reactivity is achieved by subscribing to registry version changes, causing the provider (sole caller) to re-render its subtree. Remotion's useCurrentFrame provides a secondary update path."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-003",
      "decision": "validateAndCoerceParams replaces fillDefaultParams \u2014 it coerces wrong types, not just fills defaults",
      "rationale": "Uses existing getDefaultValues as base, adds per-type coercion (number clamping, boolean check, select validation, color regex). Does not export getFallbackValue."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-004",
      "decision": "Preview uses local wrapPreviewEffect function, never mutates the global registry",
      "rationale": "The preview mirrors wrapWithClipEffects prop shapes but uses the already-compiled EffectComponent directly, avoiding shared-state coupling."
    },

      "decision": "CustomEffectEditor.tsx is dead code (no callers) \u2014 excluded from scope",
      "rationale": "Ripgrep confirmed no call sites. The plan does not migrate or modify it."

        "resolution": "Major revisions addressing 21 critique flags:\n\n**Architecture correction (FLAG-002, correctness-2, callers-1, all_locations-1/2, issue_hints-1, criteria_quality-3):** Rewrote the reactive registry approach to correctly model the provider-local \u2192 module-singleton \u2192 render-path chain. Remotion compositions additionally re-render every frame, providing a second update path.\n\n**Error boundary fix (FLAG-001, correctness-1, criteria_quality-2):** Added explicit `fallback` prop to EffectErrorBoundary. Each wrapping site passes the pre-wrapped content as fallback, so on error the boundary renders the clip without the effect. Detailed the wrapping pattern in Step 3 with correct `effectName` per category.\n\n**effectName typo fix (correctness-3):** Each boundary now uses the correct type (`clip.entrance.type`, `clip.exit.type`, `clip.continuous.type`).\n\n**Param validation upgraded (FLAG-003, issue_hints-2, callers-3, criteria_quality-1):** Replaced `fillDefaultParams` with `validateAndCoerceParams` that coerces types (number clamping, boolean check, select option validation, color regex). Uses existing `getDefaultValues` export rather than exposing `getFallbackValue`.\n\n**Preview fix (FLAG-004, correctness-4, issue_hints-3):** Abandoned global registry mutation approach. Instead, inline a `wrapPreviewEffect` local function that mirrors `wrapWithClipEffects` prop shapes. Added timeline FPS derivation from context instead of hardcoded PREVIEW_FPS.\n\n**Scope narrowing (scope-1, scope-2):** Removed CustomEffectEditor migration (dead code, no callers). Acknowledged customEffects persistence as out of scope. Removed scope creep around legacy persistence migration."
